**Delhi**,[\[b\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-25) officially the **National Capital Territory** ( **NCT**) **of Delhi**, is a [megacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megacity "Megacity") and a [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory "Union territory") of India containing [New Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi "New Delhi"), the capital of India. Straddling the [Yamuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamuna "Yamuna") river, but spread chiefly to the west, or beyond its [right bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_(geography) "Bank (geography)"), Delhi shares borders with the state of [Uttar Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh "Uttar Pradesh") in the east and with the state of [Haryana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haryana "Haryana") in the remaining directions. Delhi became a [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory "Union territory") on 1 November 1956 and the NCT in 1995.[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-statesmans-yb-2023-delhi-area-pop-26) The NCT covers an area of 1,484 square kilometres (573sqmi).[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Delhi_Info-4) According to the 2011 census, Delhi's city proper population was over 11million,[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-2011_census_Delhi-8)[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-27) while the NCT's population was about 16.8million.[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-delhi2011-10)

The topography of the medieval fort [Purana Qila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purana_Qila "Purana Qila") on the banks of the river Yamuna matches the literary description of the citadel [Indraprastha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indraprastha "Indraprastha") in the Sanskrit epic _[Mahabharata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata "Mahabharata")_; however, excavations in the area have revealed no signs of an ancient built environment.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-sunil-kumar2-28)
From the early 13th century until the mid-19th century, Delhi was the capital of two major empires, the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate "Delhi Sultanate") and the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire "Mughal Empire"), which covered large parts of [South Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asia "South Asia"). All three [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site "UNESCO World Heritage Site") in the city, the [Qutub Minar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutub_Minar "Qutub Minar"), [Humayun's Tomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humayun's_Tomb "Humayun's Tomb"), and the [Red Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Fort "Red Fort"), belong to this period. Delhi was the early centre of [Sufism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism "Sufism") and [Qawwali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qawwali "Qawwali") music. The names of [Nizamuddin Auliya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizamuddin_Auliya "Nizamuddin Auliya") and [Amir Khusrau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Khusrau "Amir Khusrau") are prominently associated with it. The [Khariboli dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khariboli_dialect "Khariboli dialect") of Delhi was part of a linguistic development that gave rise to the literature of [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu "Urdu") and later [Modern Standard Hindi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Hindi "Modern Standard Hindi"). Major Urdu poets from Delhi include [Mir Taqi Mir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir_Taqi_Mir "Mir Taqi Mir") and [Mirza Ghalib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirza_Ghalib "Mirza Ghalib"). Delhi was a notable centre of the [Indian Rebellion of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857 "Indian Rebellion of 1857"). In 1911, [New Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi "New Delhi"), a southern region within Delhi, became the capital of the [British Indian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Indian_Empire "British Indian Empire"). During the [Partition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India "Partition of India") in 1947, Delhi was transformed from a Mughal city to a Punjabi one,[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-29)[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-30) losing two-thirds of its Muslim residents, in part due to the pressure brought to bear by the arriving Hindu and Sikh refugees from western Punjab.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-31)
After independence in 1947, New Delhi continued as the capital of the [Dominion of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_India "Dominion of India"), and after 1950, of the [Republic of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_India "Republic of India").

Delhi's urban agglomeration, which includes the satellite cities [Ghaziabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaziabad "Ghaziabad"), [Faridabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faridabad "Faridabad"), [Gurgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurgaon "Gurgaon"), [Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noida "Noida"), [Greater Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Noida "Greater Noida") and [YEIDA city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YEIDA_City "YEIDA City") located in an area known as the [National Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_(India) "National Capital Region (India)") (NCR), has an estimated population of over 28million, making it the [largest metropolitan area in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_metropolitan_areas_in_India "List of metropolitan areas in India") and the [fourth-largest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_urban_areas_by_population "List of urban areas by population") (after [Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta "Jakarta"), [Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhaka "Dhaka"), and [Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo "Tokyo"), respectively).[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-32) Delhi ranks [fifth among the Indian states and union territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_territories_by_Human_Development_Index "List of Indian states and territories by Human Development Index") in [human development index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_development_index "Human development index"),[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-33) and has the [second-highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP_per_capita "List of Indian states and union territories by GDP per capita") [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_domestic_product "Gross domestic product") in India (after [Goa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goa "Goa")).[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-NSDP-16) Delhi hosted the inaugural [1951 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1951_Asian_Games "1951 Asian Games"), the [1982 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Asian_Games "1982 Asian Games"), the 1983 [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement "Non-Aligned Movement") summit, the [2010 Men's Hockey World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Men's_Hockey_World_Cup "2010 Men's Hockey World Cup"), the [2010 Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games "2010 Commonwealth Games"), the [2012 BRICS summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_BRICS_summit "4th BRICS summit"), and the [2023 G20 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_G20_New_Delhi_summit "2023 G20 New Delhi summit"), and was one of the major host cities of the [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Cricket_World_Cup "2011 Cricket World Cup") and [2023 Cricket World Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Cricket_World_Cup "2023 Cricket World Cup").

## Toponym

There are a number of myths and legends associated with the origin of the name _Delhi_. One of them is derived from _Dhillu_ or _Dilu_, a king who built a city at this location in 50BCE and named it after himself.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv1-34)[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-dhillu-35)[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-geobritish-36) Another legend holds that the name of the city is based on the [Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prakrit "Prakrit") word _dhili_ ( _loose_) and that it was used by the [Tomaras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomara_dynasty "Tomara dynasty") to refer to the city because the [iron pillar of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_pillar_of_Delhi "Iron pillar of Delhi") had a weak foundation and had to be moved.[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-geobritish-36) According to Panjab Notes and Queries, the name of the city at the time of King Prithviraj was _dilpat_, and that _dilpat_ and _dilli_ are probably derived from the old Hindi word _dil_ meaning "eminence". The former director of the [Archaeological Survey of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Survey_of_India "Archaeological Survey of India"), Alexander Cunningham, mentioned that _dilli_ later became _dihli/dehli_.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-37) Some suggest the coins in circulation in the region under the Tomaras were called _dehliwal_.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ncertVII-38) According to the [Bhavishya Purana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhavishya_Purana "Bhavishya Purana"), King Prithiviraja of [Indraprastha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indraprastha "Indraprastha") built a new fort in the modern-day [Purana Qila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purana_Qila "Purana Qila") area for the convenience of all four castes in his kingdom. He ordered the construction of a gateway to the fort and later named the fort _dehali_.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-historyhistoryabtindra-39) Some historians believe that _Dhilli_ or _Dhillika_ is the original name for the city while others believe the name could be a corruption of the [Hindustani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustani_language "Hindustani language") words _dehleez_ or _dehali_—both terms meaning "threshold" or "gateway"—and symbolic of the city as a gateway to the [Gangetic Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Gangetic_Plain "Indo-Gangetic Plain").[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-cohen-40)[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-dhilika-41)

The people of Delhi are referred to as _Delhiites_ or _Dilliwalas_.[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-42) The city is referenced in various idioms of the Northern [Indo-Aryan languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Aryan_languages "Indo-Aryan languages"). Examples include:

- _[Abhī Dillī dūr hai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilli_Door_Hai "Dilli Door Hai")_ (अभी दिल्ली दूर है / ابھی دلی دور ہے) or its [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language "Persian language") version, _Hanuz Dehli dur ast_ (هنوز دهلی دور است), literally meaning "Delhi is still far away", which is generically said about a task or journey still far from completion.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ref46cojam-43)[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ref88difob-44)
- _Ās-pās barse, Dillī pānī tarse_ (आस-पास बरसे, दिल्ली पानी तरसे / آس پاس برسے، دلی پانی ترسے), literally meaning "It pours all around, while Delhi lies parched". An allusion to the sometimes semi-arid climate of Delhi, it idiomatically refers to situations of deprivation when one is surrounded by plenty.[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ref88difob-44)

The form _Delhi_, spelled in the [Latin script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_script "Latin script") with the _h_ following the _l_, originated under colonial rule and is an alternation of the spelling based on the Urdu name of the city (دہلی, _Dehli_).[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-45)

### Ancient and early medieval periods

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Purana_Qila_ramparts%2C_Delhi.jpg/500px-Purana_Qila_ramparts%2C_Delhi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Purana_Qila_ramparts,_Delhi.jpg) The walls of the 16th-century [Purana Qila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purana_Qila "Purana Qila") built on a mound matching ancient literary descriptions[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-asher-indraprastha-46)

Traditionally seven cities have been associated with the region of Delhi. The earliest, [Indraprastha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indraprastha "Indraprastha"), is part of a literary description in the Sanskrit epic _[Mahabharata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata "Mahabharata")_ (composed c. 400BCE to 300CE but describing an earlier time[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-47)) which situates a city on a knoll on the banks of the river Yamuna. According to art historian Catherine B. Asher, the topographical description of the _Mahabharata_ matches the area of [Purana Qila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purana_Qila "Purana Qila"), a 14th-centuryCE fort of the [Delhi sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_sultanate "Delhi sultanate"), but the analogy does not go much further. Whereas the _Mahabharata_ speaks of a beautifully decorated city with surrounding fortifications, the excavations have yielded "uneven findings of [painted grey pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_Grey_Ware "Painted Grey Ware") characteristic of the eleventh century BCE; no signs of a built environment, much less fortifications, have been revealed."[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-asher-indraprastha-46)

The earliest architectural relics date back to the [Maurya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire "Maurya Empire") period (c. 300BCE); in 1966, an inscription of the Mauryan Emperor [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka "Ashoka") (273–235BCE) was discovered near Srinivaspuri. Remains of several major cities can be found in Delhi. The first of these was in the southern part of present-day Delhi. [Tomara Rajput](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomara_dynasty "Tomara dynasty") King [Anang Pal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anangpal_Tomar "Anangpal Tomar") built the [Lal Kot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qila_Rai_Pithora "Qila Rai Pithora") and several temples in 1052CE. The [Chauhan Rajputs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chahamanas_of_Shakambhari "Chahamanas of Shakambhari") under [Vigraharaja IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigraharaja_IV "Vigraharaja IV") conquered Lal Kot in the mid-12th century and renamed it [Qila Rai Pithora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qila_Rai_Pithora "Qila Rai Pithora").

### Late medieval period

See also: [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate "Delhi Sultanate")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Qutub_-_Minar%2C_Delhi_%286994969674%29.jpg/500px-Qutub_-_Minar%2C_Delhi_%286994969674%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Qutub_-_Minar,_Delhi_(6994969674).jpg) The Qutub Minar, Delhi

[Prithviraj Chauhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prithviraj_Chauhan "Prithviraj Chauhan") was defeated in 1192 by [Muhammad Ghori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_of_Ghor "Muhammad of Ghor") in the [second battle of Tarain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_battle_of_Tarain "Second battle of Tarain"). [Qutb-ud-din Aibak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutb_al-Din_Aibak "Qutb al-Din Aibak"), was given the responsibility of governing the conquered territories of India after Ghori returned to his capital, [Ghor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghor_Province "Ghor Province"). When Ghori died without an heir in 1206CE, Qutb-ud-din assumed control of Ghori's Indian possessions and laid the foundation of the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate "Delhi Sultanate") and the [Mamluk dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk_dynasty_(Delhi) "Mamluk dynasty (Delhi)"). He began construction of the [Qutb Minar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutb_Minar "Qutb Minar") and [Quwwat-al-Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutb_complex "Qutb complex") (Might of Islam) mosque, the earliest extant mosque in India. It was his successor, [Iltutmish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iltutmish "Iltutmish") (1211–1236), who consolidated the conquest of northern India.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv1-34)[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Quwwat-48) At 72.5m (238ft), the [Qutb Minar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutb_Minar "Qutb Minar"), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site "UNESCO World Heritage Site") in Delhi,[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Qutab-49) was completed during the reign of Sultan [Illtutmish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illtutmish "Illtutmish") in the 13th century. Although its style has some similarities with the [Jarkurgan minaret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarkurgan_minaret "Jarkurgan minaret"), it is more closely related to the [Ghaznavid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaznavid "Ghaznavid") and [Ghurid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghurid "Ghurid") minarets of [Central Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Asia "Central Asia")[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-mcclary-medieval-monuments-50) [Razia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Razia_Sultana "Razia Sultana"), daughter of Iltutmish, became the Sultana of Delhi upon the former's death.

For the next three hundred years, Delhi was ruled by a succession of [Turkic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_peoples "Turkic peoples"), [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayyid_Dynasty "Sayyid Dynasty") and an [Afghan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghans "Afghans"), [Lodi dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lodi_dynasty "Lodi dynasty"). They built several forts and townships that are part of the [seven cities of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Delhi "History of Delhi").[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-51) Delhi was a major centre of [Sufism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism "Sufism") during this period.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-52) The [Mamluk Sultanate (Delhi)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk_dynasty_(Delhi) "Mamluk dynasty (Delhi)") was overthrown in 1290 by [Jalal ud din Firuz Khalji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalal-ud-din_Khalji "Jalal-ud-din Khalji") (1290–1320). Under the second Khalji ruler, [Ala-ud-din Khalji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alauddin_Khalji "Alauddin Khalji"), the Delhi sultanate extended its control south of the [Narmada River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narmada_River "Narmada River") in the [Deccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan "Deccan"). The Delhi sultanate reached its greatest extent during the reign of [Muhammad bin Tughluq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Tughluq "Muhammad bin Tughluq") (1325–1351). In an attempt to bring the whole of the Deccan under control, he moved his capital to [Daulatabad, Maharashtra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daulatabad,_Maharashtra "Daulatabad, Maharashtra") in central India. However, by moving away from Delhi he lost control of the north and was forced to return to Delhi to restore order. The southern provinces then broke away. In the years following the reign of [Firoz Shah Tughlaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firoz_Shah_Tughlaq "Firoz Shah Tughlaq") (1351–1388), the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate "Delhi Sultanate") rapidly began to lose its hold over its northern provinces. Delhi was captured and sacked by [Timur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timur "Timur") in 1398,[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-53) who massacred 100,000 captive civilians.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-54) Delhi's decline continued under the [Sayyid dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayyid_dynasty "Sayyid dynasty") (1414–1451), until the sultanate was reduced to Delhi and its hinterland. Under the Afghan [Lodi dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lodi_dynasty "Lodi dynasty") (1451–1526), the sultanate recovered control of Punjab and the [Gangetic plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangetic_plain "Gangetic plain") to once again achieve domination over Northern India. However, the recovery was short-lived and the sultanate was destroyed in 1526 by [Babur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babur "Babur"), founder of the [Mughal dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire "Mughal Empire").\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

### Early modern period

See also: [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire "Mughal Empire")

[![Red Fort with the Indian Flag at the centre](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Delhi_Red_fort.jpg/500px-Delhi_Red_fort.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_Red_fort.jpg) [Red Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Fort "Red Fort"), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_India "List of World Heritage Sites in India"), was the main residence of the [Mughal emperors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Mughal_emperors "List of Mughal emperors") for nearly 200 years.

In 1526, [Babur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babur "Babur"), a descendant of [Genghis Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Descent_from_Genghis_Khan "Descent from Genghis Khan") and [Timur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timur "Timur") from the [Fergana Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fergana_Valley "Fergana Valley") in modern-day [Uzbekistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbekistan "Uzbekistan"), invaded India and defeated the last Lodhi sultan in the [First Battle of Panipat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Battle_of_Panipat "First Battle of Panipat") and founded the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire "Mughal Empire") that ruled from Delhi and [Agra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agra "Agra").[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv1-34) The Mughal dynasty ruled Delhi for more than three centuries, with a sixteen-year hiatus during the reigns of [Sher Shah Suri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sher_Shah_Suri "Sher Shah Suri") and [Hemu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemu "Hemu") from 1540 to 1556.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-shershah-55) [Shah Jahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Jahan "Shah Jahan") built the seventh city of Delhi that bears his name _[Shahjahanabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahjahanabad "Shahjahanabad")_, which served as the capital of the Mughal Empire from 1638 and is today known as the _Old City_ or _Old Delhi_.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-book11111-56)

After the death of [Aurangzeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurangzeb "Aurangzeb") in 1707, the Mughal Empire's influence declined rapidly as the Hindu [Maratha Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maratha_Empire "Maratha Empire") from [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau "Deccan Plateau") rose to prominence.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-57) In 1737, Maratha forces led by [Baji Rao I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baji_Rao_I "Baji Rao I") sacked Delhi following their victory against the Mughals in the First Battle of Delhi. In 1739, the Mughal Empire lost the huge [Battle of Karnal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Karnal "Battle of Karnal") in less than three hours against the numerically outnumbered but militarily superior Persian army led by [Nader Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nader_Shah "Nader Shah") of [Persia.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afsharid_dynasty "Afsharid dynasty") After his [invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nader_Shah's_invasion_of_India "Nader Shah's invasion of India"), he [completely sacked and looted Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sack_of_Delhi "Sack of Delhi"), carrying away immense wealth including the [Peacock Throne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacock_Throne "Peacock Throne"), the [Daria-i-Noor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daria-i-Noor "Daria-i-Noor"), and [Koh-i-Noor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koh-i-Noor "Koh-i-Noor"). The Mughals, severely further weakened, could never overcome this crushing defeat and humiliation which also left the way open for more invaders to come, including eventually the [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj "British Raj").[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-58)[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-59)[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-60) [Nader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nader_Shah "Nader Shah") eventually agreed to leave the city and India after forcing the Mughal emperor [Muhammad Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Shah "Muhammad Shah") I to beg him for mercy and granting him the keys of the city and the royal treasury.[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-61) A treaty signed in 1752 made Marathas the protectors of the Mughal throne in Delhi.[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-62) The city was sacked again in 1757 by the forces of [Ahmad Shah Durrani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Shah_Durrani "Ahmad Shah Durrani"), although it was not annexed by the [Afghan Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durrani_Empire "Durrani Empire") and being its vassal state under the Mughal emperor. Then the Marathas battled and [won](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Delhi_(1757) "Battle of Delhi (1757)") control of Delhi from the Afghans.[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-63)

### Colonial period

See also: [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj "British Raj")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/NewDelhiInaugurationSecondDayCancellation27Feb1931.jpg/500px-NewDelhiInaugurationSecondDayCancellation27Feb1931.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:NewDelhiInaugurationSecondDayCancellation27Feb1931.jpg) [British India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidencies_and_provinces_of_British_India "Presidencies and provinces of British India") stamps, inauguration, New Delhi, February 1931

In 1803, during the [Second Anglo-Maratha War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Anglo-Maratha_War "Second Anglo-Maratha War"), the forces of [British East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_East_India_Company "British East India Company") defeated the Maratha forces in the [Battle of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Delhi,_1803 "Battle of Delhi, 1803").[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-64) During the [Indian Rebellion of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857 "Indian Rebellion of 1857"), Delhi fell to the forces of East India Company after a bloody fight known as the [Siege of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Delhi "Siege of Delhi"). The city came under the direct control of the [British Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Government "British Government") in 1858. It was made a district province of the [Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab_Province_(British_India) "Punjab Province (British India)").[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv1-34) In 1911, it was announced that the capital of the [British Indian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj "British Raj") was to be transferred from [Calcutta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcutta "Calcutta") to Delhi.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-cal_to_del-65) This formally transferred on 12 December 1911.[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-66)

The name "New Delhi" was given in 1927, and the new capital was inaugurated on 13 February 1931. [New Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi "New Delhi") was officially declared as the capital of the [Union of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominion_of_India "Dominion of India") after the country gained [independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Republic_of_India "History of the Republic of India") on 15 August 1947.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-67)

### Partition and post-independence

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/New_Delhi_India_~Khan_Market.jpg/500px-New_Delhi_India_~Khan_Market.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:New_Delhi_India_~Khan_Market.jpg) [Khan Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Market "Khan Market") in New Delhi, now a high-end shopping district, was established in 1951 to help refugees of the [Partition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India "Partition of India"), especially those from the [North West Frontier Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_West_Frontier_Province "North West Frontier Province") (NWFP). It honours [Khan Abdul Jabbar Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Abdul_Jabbar_Khan "Khan Abdul Jabbar Khan"), chief minister of NWFP during the Partition.[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-lakhani-indexpress-khan-68)[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-bhardwaj-khan-reuters-69)

During the [partition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_India "Partition of India"), around five hundred thousand Hindu and Sikh refugees, mainly from [West Punjab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjab,_Pakistan "Punjab, Pakistan") migrated to Delhi, whereas around three hundred thousand Muslim residents of the city migrated to Pakistan.[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-70)[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-71) Delhi has expanded much since 1947; the small part of it that was constructed during the British period has come to be informally known as _Lutyens' Delhi_.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-lutdelhiii-72)

The [States Reorganisation Act, 1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act,_1956 "States Reorganisation Act, 1956") created the Union Territory of Delhi from its predecessor, the _[Chief Commissioner's Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Commissioner's_Province "Chief Commissioner's Province") of Delhi_.[\[1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-7thAmend56-1)[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ReorgAct56-2) The Constitution (Sixty-ninth Amendment) Act, 1991 declared the Union Territory of Delhi to be formally known as the National Capital Territory of Delhi.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-NCTact-3) The Act gave Delhi its legislative assembly along Civil lines, though with limited powers.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-NCTact-3)

Delhi was the primary site in the nationwide [anti-Sikh pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_anti-Sikh_riots "1984 anti-Sikh riots") of 1984, which resulted in the death of around 2,800 people in the city according to government figures, though independent estimates of the number of people killed tend to be higher. The riots were set off by the [assassination of Indira Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Indira_Gandhi "Assassination of Indira Gandhi")—the Prime Minister of India at the time—by her Sikh bodyguards.[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-73)

In 2001, the [Parliament of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_India "Parliament of India") building in New Delhi was [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Indian_Parliament_attack "2001 Indian Parliament attack") by armed militants, killing six security personnel.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-74) India suspected Pakistan-based Jihadist militant groups were behind the attack, which caused a major [diplomatic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001%E2%80%932002_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_standoff "2001–2002 India–Pakistan standoff") between the two countries.[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-75) There were further terrorist attacks in Delhi in [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Delhi_bombings "2005 Delhi bombings") and [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13_September_2008_Delhi_bombings "13 September 2008 Delhi bombings"), resulting in a total of 92 deaths.[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-news24.com-76)[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-77) In 2020, [Delhi witnessed worst communal violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Delhi_riots "2020 Delhi riots") in decades. The riots, caused mainly by Hindu mobs attacking Muslims,[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-guardian-3-16-20-1-78)[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-NYTimes-Analysis-March1-79) 53 people were killed, two-thirds were Muslims,[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-nytimes-2020-3-12-two-thirds-80)[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-wapo-3-6-20-slater-1-81)[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-wapo-3-2-20-slater-1-82) and the rest [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindus "Hindus").[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-wapo-3-6-20-slater-1-81)

## Geography

Main article: [Environment of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_Delhi "Environment of Delhi")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Delhi_aerial_photo_04-2016_img11.jpg/500px-Delhi_aerial_photo_04-2016_img11.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_aerial_photo_04-2016_img11.jpg) Aerial view of Delhi in April 2016 with river [Yamuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamuna "Yamuna") in top-right

Delhi is located in [North India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_India "North India") at [28°37′N77°14′E﻿ / ﻿28.61°N 77.23°E﻿ / 28.61; 77.23](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Delhi&params=28.61_N_77.23_E_). The city is bordered on its northern, western, and southern sides by the [state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_and_union_territories_of_India "States and union territories of India") of [Haryana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haryana "Haryana") and to the east by that of [Uttar Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh "Uttar Pradesh") (UP). Two prominent features of the geography of Delhi are the Yamuna flood plains and the [Delhi Ridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Ridge "Delhi Ridge"). The [Yamuna River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamuna_River "Yamuna River") was the historical boundary between [Haryana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haryana "Haryana") and UP, and its floodplains provide fertile alluvial soil suitable for agriculture but are prone to recurrent flooding. The Yamuna, a sacred river in [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism"), is the only major river flowing through Delhi. The [Hindon River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindon_River "Hindon River") separates Ghaziabad from the eastern part of Delhi. The Delhi Ridge originates in the [Aravalli Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aravalli_Range "Aravalli Range") to the south and encircles the west, northeast, and northwest parts of the city. It reaches a height of 318m (1,043ft) and is a dominant feature of the region.[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-gisridge-83)

In addition to the wetlands formed by the Yamuna River, Delhi continues to retain over 500 ponds (wetlands < 5ha (12 acres)), which in turn support a considerable number of bird species.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-:0-84) Delhi's ponds, despite experiencing ecological deterioration due to garbage dumping and concretisation, support the largest number of bird species known to be using ponds anywhere in the world.[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-:1-85) Existing policy in Delhi prevents the conversion of wetlands and, quite inadvertently, has led to the city's ponds becoming invaluable refuge for birds.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-:0-84)[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-:1-85)

The National Capital Territory of Delhi covers an area of 1,483km2 (573sqmi), of which 783km2 (302sqmi) is designated rural, and 700km2 (270sqmi) urban therefore making it the largest city in terms of area in the country. It has a maximum length of 51.9km (32mi) and a greatest width of 48.48km (30mi).[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-86)Delhi is included in India's [seismic zone-IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthquake_zones_of_India "Earthquake zones of India"), indicating its vulnerability to major earthquakes.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-hazardprofile-87) The region is traversed by the Delhi–Haridwar Ridge and the Delhi–Moradabad fault systems, which contribute to its seismic hazard, with the potential for earthquakes reaching intensity VIII on the MSK scale.[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-88)

### Climate

See also: [Climate of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Delhi "Climate of Delhi")

Delhi features a dry winter [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate "Humid subtropical climate") ( [Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification") _Cwa_), closely bordering a [hot semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_semi-arid_climate "Hot semi-arid climate") (Köppen _BSh_). The warm season lasts from Mid-Late March to mid-late June with an average daily high temperature above 39°C (102°F). The hottest day of the year is usually in late May, with an average high of 42°C (108°F) and a low of 27°C (81°F).[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-weatherspark-89) The cold season lasts from late November to late February with an average daily high temperature below 20°C (68°F). The coldest day of the year is usually in January, with an average low of 6.9°C (44.4°F) and a high of 19.3°C (66.7°F).[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-weatherspark-89) In early March, the wind direction changes from north-westerly to south-westerly. From April to October, the weather is hot. The monsoon arrives at the end of June, along with an increase in humidity.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The brief, mild winter starts in late November, peaks in January, and heavy fog often occurs.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Fog-90) Delhi receives an average annual precipitation of 774.4mm (30.49in).[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-91)
