### Air pollution

See also: [Environmental issues in Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Delhi "Environmental issues in Delhi") and [Air pollution in Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution_in_Delhi "Air pollution in Delhi")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Poulluted_killer_fog_in_Delhi.jpg/500px-Poulluted_killer_fog_in_Delhi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Poulluted_killer_fog_in_Delhi.jpg) A dense toxic smog in Delhi blocking out the sun, November 2017

According to the [World Health Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Health_Organization "World Health Organization") (WHO), Delhi was the most polluted[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-109) city in the world in 2014. In 2016, the WHO downgraded Delhi to the eleventh-worst in the urban air quality database.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-110) However, as recently as 2022, data from the WHO and [IQAir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IQAir "IQAir"), among other groups, ranked Delhi as the fourth most-polluted city globally.[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-111) According to one estimate, [air pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution "Air pollution") causes the death of about 10,500 people every year.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-TimePollution-112)[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-VOAPollution-113)[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-EconomistPollution-114) The [air quality index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_quality_index "Air quality index") is generally moderate (101–200) between January and September, and then it drastically deteriorates to Very Poor (301–400), Severe (401–500) or Hazardous (500+) levels between October and December,[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-115)[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-116) due to various factors, including [stubble burning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stubble_burning "Stubble burning") (a type of [biomass burning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combustion_of_biomass?action=edit&redlink=1 "Combustion of biomass")), firecracker-burning during [Diwali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwali "Diwali"), and cold weather.[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-117)[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-118)[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-119) During 2013–14, peak levels of fine [particulate matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Particulate_matter "Particulate matter") (PM) increased by about 44%, primarily due to high vehicular and industrial emissions, construction work and crop burning in adjoining states.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-TimePollution-112)[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-WSJPollution-120)[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-GARDINER,_25_January_14-121)[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-BEARAK,_8_feb-122) Delhi has the highest level of airborne [PM2.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PM2.5 "PM2.5")—considered most harmful to health—with 153 micrograms per million.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-123)

Rising air pollution levels have significantly increased lung-related ailments (especially [asthma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asthma "Asthma") and [lung cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lung_cancer "Lung cancer")) among Delhi's children and women.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-TodayPollution-124)[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-DNAPollution-125) The dense [smog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smog "Smog") and [haze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haze "Haze") during winter results in major air and rail traffic disruptions every year.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ReutersPollution-126) According to Indian meteorologists, the average maximum temperature during winters has declined notably since 1998 due to rising air pollution.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-127)

India's [Ministry of Earth Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Earth_Sciences "Ministry of Earth Sciences") published a research paper in October 2018 attributing almost 41% of PM2.5 air pollution in Delhi to vehicular emissions, 21.5% to dust/fire and 18% to industries.[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-MoES5-128) The director of the [Centre for Science and Environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_Science_and_Environment "Centre for Science and Environment") (CSE) alleged that the [Society of Indian Automobile Manufacturers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_India "Automotive industry in India") (SIAM) is lobbied "against the report" because it was "inconvenient" to the automobile industry.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-CSE-129) Environmentalists have also criticised the Delhi government for not doing enough to curb air pollution and to inform people about air quality issues.[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-VOAPollution-113) In 2014, an environmental panel appealed to [India's Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India's_Supreme_Court "India's Supreme Court") to impose a 30% [cess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cess "Cess") on diesel cars, but till date no action has been taken to penalise the automobile industry.[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-130)

Most Delhi residents are unaware of the alarming levels of air pollution in the city and the health risks associated with it.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-GARDINER,_25_January_14-121)[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-BEARAK,_8_feb-122) In 2020, annual average [PM2.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PM2.5 "PM2.5") in the city stood at 107.6μg/m3, which is almost 21.5 times the WHO's PM2.5 Guideline (5μg/m3; set in September 2021).[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-auto-131) These pollution levels are estimated to reduce the [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy "Life expectancy") of an average person living in Delhi by almost 10.1 years.[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-auto-131)

However, as of 2015[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit), awareness, particularly among the foreign diplomatic community and high-income Indians, was noticeably increasing.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-NYT021415-132) Since the mid-1990s, Delhi has undertaken some measures to curb air pollution—it has the third-highest quantity of trees among Indian cities;[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-133) the [Delhi Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Transport_Corporation "Delhi Transport Corporation") operates the world's largest fleet of environmentally friendly [compressed natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compressed_natural_gas "Compressed natural gas") (CNG) buses.[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-cities.expressindia.com-134) In 1996, the CSE started a public interest litigation in the [Supreme Court of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_India "Supreme Court of India") that ordered the conversion of Delhi's fleet of buses and taxis to run on CNG and banned the use of [leaded petrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaded_petrol "Leaded petrol") in 1998. In 2003, Delhi won the [United States Department of Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Energy "United States Department of Energy")'s first 'Clean Cities International Partner of the Year' award for its "bold efforts to curb air pollution and support alternative fuel initiatives".[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-cities.expressindia.com-134) The [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro") has also been credited for significantly reducing air pollutants in the city.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-135)

However, according to several authors, most of these gains have been lost, especially due to [stubble burning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stubble_burning "Stubble burning"), a rise in the market share of [diesel cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_exhaust "Diesel exhaust"), and a considerable decline in bus ridership.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-kumari-136)[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-CSEPollution-137) According to CSE and System of Air Quality Weather Forecasting and Research (SAFAR), burning of agricultural waste in nearby Punjab, Haryana, and Uttar Pradesh regions results in severe intensification of smog over Delhi.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ETTPollution-138)[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-CSE2Pollution-139)

Delhi has been ranked 7th best "National Clean Air City" (under Category 1 >10L Population cities) in India according to 'Swachh Vayu Survekshan 2024 Results' [\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-140)

According to the World Air Quality Report 2024, Delhi remains the world's most polluted capital city.[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-141)

## Civic administration

See also: [List of districts of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Delhi "List of districts of Delhi"), [List of neighbourhoods of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_neighbourhoods_of_Delhi "List of neighbourhoods of Delhi"), and [List of towns in National Capital Territory of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_towns_in_National_Capital_Territory_of_Delhi "List of towns in National Capital Territory of Delhi")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Delhi_Municipalities_2022.svg/500px-Delhi_Municipalities_2022.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_Municipalities_2022.svg) Map of the three municipal corporations of Delhi[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Delhi_Municipalities.svg/500px-Delhi_Municipalities.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_Municipalities.svg) Map of the former six municipal corporations of Delhi (2011–2022)

Currently, the NCT of Delhi is made up of one [division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_India#Divisions "Administrative divisions of India"), [11 districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Delhi "List of districts of Delhi"), 33 [subdivisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_India#Subdistricts "Administrative divisions of India"), 59census towns, and 300villages.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-142)
On the other hand, the NCT of Delhi is divided into three municipalities. The boundaries of municipalities may be different from district boundaries:

1. [Municipal Corporation of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporation_of_Delhi "Municipal Corporation of Delhi") (MCD), which occupies an area of 1,397.3km2 (539.5sqmi) and is sub-divided into 12 zones, that is, Centre, South, West, Najafgarh, Rohini, Civil Lines, Karol Bagh, SP-City, Keshavpuram, Narela, Shahdara North and Shahdara South.[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-mcdonline-143) [Pravesh Wahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pravesh_Wahi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Pravesh Wahi"), is the current [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor "Mayor") of the unified [Municipal Corporation of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporation_of_Delhi "Municipal Corporation of Delhi") since 2026.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-144)
2. [New Delhi Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi "New Delhi"), which occupies an area of 42.7km2 (16.5sqmi)
3. [Delhi Cantonment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Cantonment "Delhi Cantonment"), which occupies an area of 42.3km2 (16.3sqmi)

Between 13 January 2011 and 22 May 2022, MCD was divided into three municipal corporations:[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-145)

1. [South Delhi Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Delhi_Municipal_Corporation "South Delhi Municipal Corporation") (SDMC) had jurisdiction over South and West Delhi areas, including Mahipalpur, Rajouri Garden, Uttam Nagar, Badarpur, Jaitpur, Janakpuri, Hari Nagar, Tilak Nagar, Dwarka, Jungpura, Greater Kailash, R K Puram, Malviya Nagar, Kalkaji, Ambedkar Nagar and Pul pehladpur.
2. [North Delhi Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Delhi_Municipal_Corporation "North Delhi Municipal Corporation") (NDMC) had jurisdiction over areas such as Badli, Rithala, Bawana, Kirari, Mangolpuri, Tri Nagar, Model Town, Sadar Bazar, Chandni Chowk, Matia Mahal, Karol Bagh, Moti Nagar
3. [East Delhi Municipal Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Delhi_Municipal_Corporation "East Delhi Municipal Corporation") (EDMC) had jurisdiction over areas such as Patparganj, Kondli, Laxmi Nagar, Seemapuri, Gonda, Karawal Nagar, Babarpur and Shahadra.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-146)

Delhi is home to the [High Court of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_High_Court "Delhi High Court"). The High Court of Delhi is the highest in the Delhi before Supreme Court. The High Court of Delhi just like the apex court and other High Courts in India is the Court of record. Delhi is also home to various District Court according to jurisdictions. Delhi have Currently seven [District Courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_courts_of_India "District courts of India") namely Tis Hazari Court Complex, Karkardooma Court Complex, Patiala House Court Complex, Rohini Court Complex, Dwarka Courts Complex, Saket Court Complex, and Rouse Avenue Court
Apart from the District Courts Delhi also have Consumer Courts, CBI Courts, Labour Courts, Revenue Courts, Army tribunals, electricity tribunals, Railway Tribunals, and other various tribunals situated according to appropriate jurisdictions.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-147)[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-148)

For policing purposes Delhi is divided into fifteen [police districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Delhi_Police "Districts of Delhi Police") which are further subdivided into 95 local police station zones. Delhi currently has 180 police stations.[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-policestations-149)[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-150)

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Delhi "Government of Delhi") and [Government of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India "Government of India")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Delhi_Vidhan_Sabha.jpg/500px-Delhi_Vidhan_Sabha.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_Vidhan_Sabha.jpg) [Delhi Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Legislative_Assembly "Delhi Legislative Assembly")

As a first-level administrative division, the National Capital Territory of Delhi has its own [Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Delhi "Legislative Assembly of Delhi"), [Lieutenant Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lieutenant_governors_of_Delhi "List of lieutenant governors of Delhi"), the council of ministers, and [Chief Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Delhi "Chief Minister of Delhi"). Members of the legislative assembly are directly elected from territorial constituencies in the NCT. The legislative assembly was abolished in 1956, after which direct federal control was implemented until it was re-established in 1993. The municipal corporation handles civic administration for the city as part of the [Panchayati Raj Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayati_Raj_Act "Panchayati Raj Act"). The [Government of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India "Government of India") and the [Government of National Capital Territory of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Delhi "Government of Delhi") jointly administer New Delhi, where both bodies are located. The [Parliament of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_India "Parliament of India"), the [Rashtrapati Bhavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashtrapati_Bhavan,_New_Delhi "Rashtrapati Bhavan, New Delhi") (Presidential Palace), [Cabinet Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Secretariat_of_India "Cabinet Secretariat of India"), and the Supreme Court of India are located in the municipal district of New Delhi. There are 70 assembly constituencies and seven [Lok Sabha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lok_Sabha "Lok Sabha") (Indian Parliament's lower house) constituencies in Delhi.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-assmbconst-151)[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-loksabhaconst-152)

In the first legislative assembly elections, [Bharatiya Janata Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party "Bharatiya Janata Party") (BJP), led by [Madan Lal Khurana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madan_Lal_Khurana "Madan Lal Khurana"), came to power.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-POD-153) In 1998, the Congress came to power under the leadership of [Sheila Dikshit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheila_Dikshit "Sheila Dikshit"), who was subsequently re-elected for 3 consecutive terms. But in [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Delhi_Legislative_Assembly_election "2013 Delhi Legislative Assembly election"), the Congress was ousted from power by the newly formed [Aam Aadmi Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aam_Aadmi_Party "Aam Aadmi Party") (AAP) led by [Arvind Kejriwal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arvind_Kejriwal "Arvind Kejriwal") forming the government with outside support from the Congress. This was despite the [Bharatiya Janata Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party "Bharatiya Janata Party") winning a plurity.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-154) However, that government was short-lived, collapsing only after 49 days.[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-155) Delhi was then under [President's rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President's_rule "President's rule") until February 2015,[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-156) after which AAP returned to power after a landslide victory, winning 67 out of the 70 seats in the [Delhi Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Legislative_Assembly "Delhi Legislative Assembly").[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-157) AAP has held power until Feb 2025.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-158) Following [assembly elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Legislative_Assembly "Delhi Legislative Assembly") in 2025, [BJP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party "Bharatiya Janata Party") came to power with an outright majority.[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-159)

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Delhi "Economy of Delhi")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/Dry_fruits_being_sold_at_Khari_Baoli_market_in_Old_Delhi.jpg/500px-Dry_fruits_being_sold_at_Khari_Baoli_market_in_Old_Delhi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dry_fruits_being_sold_at_Khari_Baoli_market_in_Old_Delhi.jpg)

The [Khari Baoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khari_Baoli "Khari Baoli") market in Old Delhi is one of the oldest and busiest in the city.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Connaught_Place_New_Delhi.jpg/500px-Connaught_Place_New_Delhi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Connaught_Place_New_Delhi.jpg)

[Connaught Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connaught_Place,_New_Delhi "Connaught Place, New Delhi") in New Delhi is an important economic hub of the [National Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_(India) "National Capital Region (India)").

Delhi is the largest commercial center in northern India. As of 2016[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit) recent estimates of the economy of the Delhi urban area have ranged from $370billion to $400billion ( [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity "Purchasing power parity") [metro GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product "Gross metropolitan product")) ranking it either the [most or second-most productive metro area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_GDP "List of cities by GDP") of India.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-gdp-160)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-metrogdp-161) The nominal [GSDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_and_union_territories_by_GDP "List of Indian states and union territories by GDP") of the NCT of Delhi for 2016–17 was estimated at ₹6,224 billion (US$65billion), 13% higher than in 2015–16.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Budget_Analysis-162) Oxford Economics Global Cities index 2024 ranked Delhi as best city in India and 108th best city in the world in Economics Category.

As per the Economic survey of Delhi (2005–2006), the [tertiary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_industry "Tertiary sector of industry") contributes 70.95% of Delhi's gross SDP followed by [secondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sector_of_industry "Secondary sector of industry") and [primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_sector_of_industry "Primary sector of industry") sectors with 25.20% and 3.85% contributions, respectively.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv2-163) Delhi's workforce constitutes 32.82% of the population, and increased by 52.52% between 1991 and 2001.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv5-164) Delhi's unemployment rate decreased from 12.57% in 1999–2000 to 4.63% in 2003.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv5-164) In December 2004, 636,000 people were registered with various employment exchange programmes in Delhi.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv5-164)

In 2018 the total workforce in national and state governments and the quasi-government sector was 594,000, and the private sector employed 273,000.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-165) Key service industries are information technology, telecommunications, hotels, banking, media and tourism.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-166) Construction, power, health and community services and real estate are also important to the city's economy. Delhi has one of India's largest and fastest growing retail industries.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-167) Manufacturing also grew considerably as consumer goods companies established manufacturing units and headquarters in the city. Delhi's large consumer market and the availability of skilled labour has also attracted foreign investment. In 2001, the manufacturing sector employed 1,440,000 workers and the city had 129,000 industrial units.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv9-168)

## Utility services

See also: [Bhalswa landfill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhalswa_landfill "Bhalswa landfill")

Delhi's municipal water supply is managed by the [Delhi Jal Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Jal_Board "Delhi Jal Board") (DJB). As of November2025[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit), it supplies 900million gallons per day (MGD).[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-169) The shortfall is met by private and public [tube wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tube_well "Tube well") and [hand pumps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_pump "Hand pump"). At 240MGD, the Bhakra storage is DJB's largest water source, followed by the Yamuna and [Ganges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganges "Ganges") rivers. Delhi's [groundwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundwater "Groundwater") level is falling and its population density is increasing, so residents often encounter acute water shortage.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv13-170) Research on Delhi suggests that up to half of the city's water use is unofficial groundwater.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-171)

In Delhi, daily domestic solid waste production is 8000 [tonnes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonne "Tonne") which is dumped at three [landfill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landfill "Landfill") locations by MCD.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-hinduwaste-172) The daily domestic waste water production is 470MGD and industrial waste water is 70MGD.[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Delhirisks-173) A large portion of the sewage flows untreated into the Yamuna river.[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Delhirisks-173)

The city's electricity consumption is about 1,265kWh per capita but the actual demand is higher.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv11-174) In Delhi power distribution is managed by [TPDDL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Power_Delhi_Distribution_Limited "Tata Power Delhi Distribution Limited") and [BSES Yamuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSES_Yamuna_Power_Limited "BSES Yamuna Power Limited") & [BSES Rajdhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BSES_Rajdhani "BSES Rajdhani") since 2002, transmission of power is done by [Delhi Transco Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Transco_Limited "Delhi Transco Limited") and [Powergrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powergrid "Powergrid"), while generation of power is by [IPGCL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPGCL "IPGCL") and [PPCL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PPCL?action=edit&redlink=1 "PPCL"). The city also imports a significant quantum of power from other states.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-175)

The [Delhi Fire Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Fire_Service "Delhi Fire Service") runs 43 fire stations that attend about 15,000 fire and rescue calls per year.[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-dfs-176) The state-owned [MTNL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTNL "MTNL") and private enterprises such as [Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel "Bharti Airtel"), [Vi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea "Vodafone Idea"), [Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jio "Jio") provide telephone and cell phone services to the city. Cellular coverage is available in [GSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSM "GSM"), [3G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3G "3G"), [4G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4G "4G"), [4G+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LTE_Advanced "LTE Advanced") and [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G "5G").\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

## Transport

Main article: [Transport in Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Delhi "Transport in Delhi")

### Air

[![ Shown here is the check-in counter at Terminal 3 of the airport.](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Mudras_at_Indira_Gandhi_Delhi_1007.jpg/500px-Mudras_at_Indira_Gandhi_Delhi_1007.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mudras_at_Indira_Gandhi_Delhi_1007.jpg) [Indira Gandhi International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indira_Gandhi_International_Airport "Indira Gandhi International Airport")'s immigration counter in Terminal 3[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-177)

[Indira Gandhi International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indira_Gandhi_International_Airport "Indira Gandhi International Airport"), situated to the south-west of Delhi, is the main gateway for the city's domestic and international civilian air traffic. In 2015–16, the airport handled more than 48million passengers,[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-178) making it the busiest airport in India and South Asia. Terminal 3, which cost ₹96.8 billion (US$1.0billion) to construct between 2007 and 2010, handles an additional 37million passengers annually.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-179) In 2010, IGIA was conferred the 4th best airport award in the world in the 15–25million category, by [Airports Council International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airports_Council_International "Airports Council International"). The airport was rated as the _Best airport in the world_ in the 25–40million passengers category in 2015, by Airports Council International. Delhi Airport was awarded _The Best Airport in Central Asia_ and _Best Airport Staff in Central Asia_ at the [Skytrax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skytrax "Skytrax") World Airport Awards 2015.[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-180)[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-181) [Hindon Domestic Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindon_Airport "Hindon Airport") in Ghaziabad was inaugurated by Prime Minister Narendra Modi as the second airport for the Delhi-NCR Region on 8 March 2019.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-182) A second international airport open for commercial flights has been suggested either by expansion of [Meerut Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meerut_Airport "Meerut Airport") or construction of a new airport in [Greater Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Noida "Greater Noida").[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-183)
The [Taj International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taj_International_Airport "Taj International Airport") project in [Jewar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewar "Jewar") has been approved by the [Uttar Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh "Uttar Pradesh") government.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-184)

The _Delhi Flying Club_, established in 1928 with two [de Havilland Moth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Havilland_Moth "De Havilland Moth") aircraft named _Delhi_ and _Roshanara_, was based at [Safdarjung Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safdarjung_Airport "Safdarjung Airport") which started operations in 1929, when it was the Delhi's only airport and the second in India.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-mecca-185) The airport functioned until 2001; however, in January 2002 the government closed the airport for flying activities because of security concerns following [the New York attacks in September 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11 "9/11"). Since then, the club only carries out aircraft maintenance courses and is used for helicopter rides to Indira Gandhi International Airport for VIP including the president and the prime minister.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-mecca-185)[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-186)

### Road

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Delhi_Metro_and_CNG_Buses_in_Azadpur_Neighborhood.jpg/500px-Delhi_Metro_and_CNG_Buses_in_Azadpur_Neighborhood.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delhi_Metro_and_CNG_Buses_in_Azadpur_Neighborhood.jpg)

[Compressed natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compressed_natural_gas "Compressed natural gas") red- and green buses have low floors; orange has standard.[\[c\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-188)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Old_Delhi_rickshaw_2011.JPG/500px-Old_Delhi_rickshaw_2011.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Old_Delhi_rickshaw_2011.JPG)

The [cycle rickshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycle_rickshaw "Cycle rickshaw") and the [auto rickshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_rickshaw "Auto rickshaw") are commonly used in Delhi for travelling short distances.

Delhi has the highest road density of 2103km/100km2 in India.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-IND1-189) It is connected to other parts of India by five [National Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_highways_of_India "National highways of India"): [NH 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_1_(India,_old_numbering) "National Highway 1 (India, old numbering)"), [NH 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_2_(India,_old_numbering) "National Highway 2 (India, old numbering)"), [NH 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_8_(India,_old_numbering) "National Highway 8 (India, old numbering)"), [NH 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_10_(India,_old_numbering) "National Highway 10 (India, old numbering)") and [NH 24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_24_(India) "National Highway 24 (India)"). The Delhi–Mumbai and Delhi–Kolkata prongs of the [Golden Quadrilateral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Quadrilateral "Golden Quadrilateral") start from the city. The majority of the city's roads which are 60ft (18m) wide or above are maintained by the Public Works Department (PWD)[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-190) which is under the jurisdiction of the [Government of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Delhi "Government of Delhi") while some are maintained by [Delhi Development Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Development_Authority "Delhi Development Authority") and [New Delhi Municipal Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi_Municipal_Council "New Delhi Municipal Council")[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-roadauth-191) which are under the jurisdiction of the [Government of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India "Government of India"). Roads and streets less than 60ft (18m) wide are maintained by the [Municipal Corporation of Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Corporation_of_Delhi "Municipal Corporation of Delhi"). Roads and streets in unauthorised colonies are maintained by the local [Member of the Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_Legislative_Assembly_(India) "Member of the Legislative Assembly (India)").[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-192)

Buses are the most popular means of road transport, catering to about 60% of Delhi's total demand.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ET-193) Delhi has one of India's largest bus transport systems. In 1998, the Supreme Court of India ruled that all public transport vehicles in Delhi must be fuelled by [compressed natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compressed_natural_gas "Compressed natural gas") (CNG) to tackle increasing vehicular pollution.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-supremelaw-194) The state-owned [Delhi Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Transport_Corporation "Delhi Transport Corporation") (DTC) is a major bus service provider which operates the world's largest fleet of CNG-fuelled buses.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-DTC-195)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ND-196) In addition, cluster scheme buses are operated by [Delhi Integrated Multi-Modal Transit System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Integrated_Multi-Modal_Transit_System "Delhi Integrated Multi-Modal Transit System") (DIMTS) with the participation of private concessionaires and DTC.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-197)[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-198) In December 2017, the DTC and cluster buses carried over 4.19million passengers per day.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-199) [Kashmiri Gate ISBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharana_Pratap_Inter_State_Bus_Terminus "Maharana Pratap Inter State Bus Terminus"), [Anand Vihar ISBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swami_Vivekanand_Inter_State_Bus_Terminus "Swami Vivekanand Inter State Bus Terminus") and [Sarai Kale Khan ISBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarai_Kale_Khan_Inter-State_Bus_Terminus "Sarai Kale Khan Inter-State Bus Terminus") are the main bus terminals for outstation buses plying to neighbouring states. Delhi's rapid rate of economic development and population growth has resulted in an increasing demand for transport, creating excessive pressure on the city's transport infrastructure. To meet the transport demand, the State and Union government constructed a mass rapid transit system, including the Delhi Metro.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv12-200) [Delhi Bus Rapid Transit System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Bus_Rapid_Transit_System "Delhi Bus Rapid Transit System") runs between [Ambedkar Nagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambedkar_Nagar "Ambedkar Nagar") and [Delhi Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Gate,_Delhi "Delhi Gate, Delhi"). As per February 2024, Delhi has around 1,650 [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus "Electric bus") managed by the [Delhi Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Transport_Corporation "Delhi Transport Corporation"), the highest in India and the third highest in the world after [Shenzhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhen "Shenzhen") and [Santiago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago "Santiago").[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-201)[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-202)

Personal vehicles, especially cars also form a major chunk of vehicles plying on Delhi roads. As of 2007[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit), private vehicles account for 30% of the total demand for transport.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv12-200) Delhi has the highest number of registered cars compared to any other metropolitan city in India.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-203) Taxis, auto rickshaws, and cycle rickshaws also ply on Delhi roads in large numbers. As of 2008[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit), the number of vehicles in the metropolitan region, Delhi NCR, was 11.2million (11.2million).[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-204) In 2008, there were 85 cars in Delhi for every 1,000 of its residents.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-205) In 2017, the number of vehicles in Delhi city alone crossed the ten million mark with the transport department of [Delhi Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Delhi "Government of Delhi") putting the total number of registered vehicles at 10,567,712 until 25 May of the year.[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-206)

### Railway

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/New_Delhi_railway_station_2.jpg/500px-New_Delhi_railway_station_2.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:New_Delhi_railway_station_2.jpg) A passenger train at the [New Delhi railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi_railway_station "New Delhi railway station"). Freight awaits pick up or transportation to other destinations.

Delhi is a major junction in the Indian railway network and is the headquarters of the [Northern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Railway_(India) "Northern Railway (India)"). The main railway stations are [New Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Delhi_railway_station "New Delhi railway station"), [Old Delhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Junction_Railway_Station "Delhi Junction Railway Station"), [Hazrat Nizamuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hazrat_Nizamuddin_Railway_Station "Hazrat Nizamuddin Railway Station"), [Anand Vihar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anand_Vihar_Railway_Terminal "Anand Vihar Railway Terminal"), [Delhi Sarai Rohilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sarai_Rohilla_railway_station "Delhi Sarai Rohilla railway station") and [Delhi Cantt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Cantonment_railway_station "Delhi Cantonment railway station").[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv12-200) The [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro"), a mass rapid transit system built and operated by [Delhi Metro Rail Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro_Rail_Corporation "Delhi Metro Rail Corporation") (DMRC), serves many parts of Delhi and the neighbouring cities [Ghaziabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaziabad "Ghaziabad"), [Faridabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faridabad "Faridabad"), [Gurgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurgaon "Gurgaon") and [Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noida "Noida").[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-207)As of December2021[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Delhi&action=edit), the metro consists of [ten operational lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Delhi_Metro_lines "List of Delhi Metro lines") with a total length of 348.12km (216.31mi) and [254 stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Delhi_metro_stations "List of Delhi metro stations"), and several other lines are under construction.[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-tie-10aug18-208) The Phase-I was built at a cost of US$2.3billion and the Phase-II was expected to cost an additional ₹216 billion (US$2.3billion).[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Bloomberg.com:_Opinion-209) Phase-II has a total length of 128km (80mi) and was completed by 2010.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Economictimes.indiatimes.com-210) [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro") completed 10years of operation on 25 December 2012. It carries millions of passengers every day.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-10_years_of_Delhi_Metro-211) In addition to the Delhi Metro, a suburban railway, the [Delhi Suburban Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Suburban_Railway "Delhi Suburban Railway") exists.[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Indian_Express-212)

### Metro

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/DelhiMetroBlueLineBombardier.jpg/500px-DelhiMetroBlueLineBombardier.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:DelhiMetroBlueLineBombardier.jpg) [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro") is widely used in the [NCR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_(India) "National Capital Region (India)").

The [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro") is a rapid transit system serving Delhi, [Ghaziabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaziabad "Ghaziabad"), [Faridabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faridabad "Faridabad"), [Gurgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurgaon "Gurgaon") and [Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noida "Noida") in the National Capital Region of India. It is the world's tenth-largest metro system by length of lines. It was India's second modern public transportation system. The network consists of [10 colour-coded lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#Network)[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-present_network-213) serving [255 stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Delhi_Metro_stations "List of Delhi Metro stations")[\[d\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-fn1-216) with a total length of 348.12 kilometres (216.31mi).[\[e\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-fn2-217) The system has a mix of underground, at-grade, and elevated stations using both [broad-gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad-gauge_railway "Broad-gauge railway") and [standard-gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard-gauge_railway "Standard-gauge railway"). All stations have escalators, lifts, and tactile tiles to guide the visually impaired from station entrances to trains. There are 18 designated parking sites at the Metro stations. In March 2010, [DMRC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro_Rail_Corporation "Delhi Metro Rail Corporation") partnered with Google India (through Google Transit) to provide train schedule and route information to mobile devices with Google Maps. It has a combination of elevated, at-grade, and underground lines, and uses both broad gauge and standard gauge rolling stock. Four types of rolling stock are used: Mitsubishi–ROTEM Broad gauge, Bombardier MOVIA, Mitsubishi–ROTEM Standard gauge, and CAF Beasain Standard gauge. The Phase-I of Delhi Metro was built for US$2.3billion and the Phase-II was expected to cost an additional ₹216 billion (US$2.3billion).[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Bloomberg.com:_Opinion-209) Phase-II has a total length of 128km (80mi) and was completed by 2010.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Economictimes.indiatimes.com-210) [Delhi Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro "Delhi Metro") completed 10years of operation on 25 December 2012. It carries millions of passengers every day.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-10_years_of_Delhi_Metro-211)

Although the Delhi Metro was built and is operated by the [Delhi Metro Rail Corporation Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Metro_Rail_Corporation_Limited "Delhi Metro Rail Corporation Limited") (DMRC), a state-owned company with equal equity participation of the governments of India and Delhi, it is under the administrative control of the Indian government's [Ministry of Urban Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Housing_and_Urban_Affairs "Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs"). Besides the construction and operation of the Delhi Metro, DMRC is also involved in the planning and implementation of metro rail, monorail, and high-speed rail projects in India and providing consultancy services to other metro projects in the country as well as abroad.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Historical population

### Population growth

According to the [2011 census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_census_of_India "2011 census of India"), the population of the NCT of Delhi is 16,753,235.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-censusindia1-219) The corresponding population density was 11,297 persons per km2 with a sex ratio of 866 women per 1000 men, and a literacy rate of 86.34%. In 2004, the birth rate, death rate and infant mortality rate per 1000 population were 20.03, 5.59 and 13.08, respectively.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv3-220) In 2001, the population of Delhi increased by 285,000 as a result of migration and by 215,000 as a result of natural population growth,[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-ecosurv3-220) which made Delhi one of the fastest-growing cities in the world. [Dwarka Sub City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwarka,_Delhi "Dwarka, Delhi"), Asia's largest planned residential area, is located within the National Capital Territory of Delhi.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-221)

[Urban expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl "Urban sprawl") has resulted in Delhi's urban area now being considered as extending beyond the NCT boundaries to incorporate the towns and cities of neighbouring states including [Faridabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faridabad "Faridabad") and [Gurgaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurgaon "Gurgaon") in [Haryana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haryana "Haryana"), and [Ghaziabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghaziabad "Ghaziabad") and [Noida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noida "Noida") in [Uttar Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh "Uttar Pradesh"), the total population of which is estimated by the United Nations to be over 28 million. According to the UN this makes Delhi urban area the world's [second-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_urban_areas_by_population "List of urban areas by population") urban area after Tokyo,[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-UNcities2018-11) although [Demographia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographia "Demographia") declares the [Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta "Jakarta") urban area to be the second-largest.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Demographia-222)

The 2011 census provided two figures for urban area population: 16,314,838 within the NCT boundary,[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Delhiuapop2011-223) and 21,753,486 for the _Extended Urban Area_.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-pibmumbai1-224) The 2021 regional plan released by the [Government of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_India "Government of India") renamed the _Extended Urban Area_ from _Delhi Metropolitan Area (DMA)_ as defined by the 2001 plan,[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-DMA-225) to _[Central National Capital Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Capital_Region_(India)#Central_National_Capital_Region "National Capital Region (India)") (CNCR)_.[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-DMA-225)[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-226)

### Slums

As of 2012, around 49% of the population of Delhi lives in slums and unauthorised colonies without any civic amenities.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-227) The majority of these slums have inadequate provisions to the basic facilities and according to a DUSIB report.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-228)

### Religions

[Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism") is Delhi's predominant religious faith, with 81.68% of Delhi's population, followed by [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam") (12.86%), [Sikhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikhism "Sikhism") (3.40%), [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism "Jainism") (0.99%), Christianity (0.87%), and [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism "Buddhism") (0.11%).[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Religion_PCA_2011-229) Other minority religions include [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism "Zoroastrianism"), [Baháʼísm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith "Baháʼí Faith") and Judaism.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-Lonelyplanet-230)[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi#cite_note-231)
